## Operação Prato (Operation Saucer)

Operação Prato (Operation Saucer) is a program on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1977-10-20 to 1978; axes C2 A2 D2; 9 connections; in the research band.

In one line: Brazilian Air Force I COMAR (Belem/PA) intelligence-section operation, Colares, Para, 1977-1978; documents held in the Arquivo Nacional Fundo OVNI, BR DFANBSB ARX.

### What it is

Operacao Prato, literally Operation Plate and usually rendered Operation Saucer, was a field operation mounted by the Forca Aerea Brasileira in the state of Para in 1977 and 1978, centred on Colares and neighbouring riverine communities on the Atlantic coast of the Amazon delta. It was commanded by Capitao Uyrange Bolivar Soares Nogueira de Hollanda Lima, who named it, and ran under I COMAR with involvement from the Servico Nacional de Informacoes. Its stated object was to investigate a wave of reports of luminous objects that residents said projected beams of light at them, a phenomenon locals called the chupa-chupa.

### Why it is implicated

Operacao Prato is the specific thing the United States has never produced: a military UAP field operation with a name, a commanding officer identified by rank and full name, a defined area of operations, dated phases, a case count, a written final report, and a paper trail that a member of the public can now request from a civilian archive. The US analogue would be if Project Blue Book had deployed officers into a county for two months with cameras, and the resulting negatives, logs and the captain's signed report were downloadable from NARA. Everything about the operation that would in the American context be cited as a reason for permanent classification, that it was run under a military dictatorship, that it involved a domestic intelligence service, that it concerned civilians who claimed physical injury, and that it produced imagery, turned out not to prevent release. That is the contrast. The concealment argument in the US rests on the proposition that this class of record cannot be released; Brazil released this class of record, and the state did not fall.

### The longer account

In the second half of 1977 the coastal and riverine municipalities of Para began generating a volume of reports that local authorities could not absorb. Residents described luminous objects hovering over houses at night and directing narrow beams of light at people, and a hospital in Colares filled with patients presenting with burns, puncture-like marks and profound weakness. The mayor of Colares appealed for help. What the Brazilian state did next is what puts this node on the board: it sent the air force.

Operacao Prato was organised out of I COMAR, the First Regional Air Command in Belem, and placed under Capitao Uyrange Hollanda. It ran in phases across the last months of 1977 and into 1978, with a team on the ground carrying cameras and observation logs, and it operated in coordination with the Servico Nacional de Informacoes, the dictatorship's civilian intelligence service. Case counts in the surviving documentation are substantial. Material now in the Arquivo Nacional includes a compilation described as Registros de Observacoes de OVNI, drawn up from data gathered by I COMAR on the recommendation of the Minister of Aeronautics, forwarding 130 OVNI records catalogued between 2 September 1977 and 28 November 1978. Alongside the paper there are photographs and drawings, and elsewhere in the same fund the archive holds audio recordings of exchanges between pilots and control tower operators.

The written product of the operation was a report signed by Hollanda that recorded no evidence of extraterrestrials. That report is the operation's official position and, on the board's own method, it is the document that must be handled as an artefact rather than as an answer. It is dated, it is signed, it is attributable, and it was produced by an officer inside a chain of command under a military government. Twenty years later Hollanda repudiated it. In August 1997, at Cabo Frio in Rio de Janeiro state, he gave a long interview to the researchers A. J. Gevaerd and Marco Petit for Revista UFO in which he described what he said the team had actually encountered in the field, including close-quarters encounters, and described unexplained phenomena in his own home afterwards. On 2 October 1997 he was found dead in his apartment at Cabo Frio. The coroner recorded death by asphyxiation due to hanging and the death was ruled suicide.

The temptation with a witness who recants a signed military report and dies weeks later is obvious, and the record does not support taking it. Gevaerd, who had spent the hours of the interview with him and who has every professional incentive to say otherwise, has stated flatly that no one 'suicided' him for talking, that Hollanda acted for his own reasons, and that Hollanda had disclosed a previous attempt on his own life, a fall from the fourth floor of a building. The honest formulation is that the board has a commanding officer who signed a negative report, publicly contradicted it two decades later, and died by his own hand within weeks, and that the person best placed to allege foul play declines to. That is a knot, not a proof, and it belongs in the open questions.

The archival afterlife is where the contrast with the US becomes concrete. In April 2009 the Air Force's documentation centre, CENDOC, sent the first tranche of Operacao Prato material to the Arquivo Nacional. Later in 2009 the Gabinete de Seguranca Institucional of the Presidency released dozens of pages of former SNI documents on the same operation. The two streams converged into the collection coded BR DFANBSB ARX, the Fundo OVNI, where they sit digitised and searchable through SIAN, the Arquivo Nacional's online information system. The mechanism that made this routine was administrative rather than political: Portaria no 551/GC3 of August 2010 established that the Aeronautica registers and forwards, and the archive publishes.

What Operacao Prato does not do is establish recovery or reverse-engineering. There is no crash, no hardware, no custody chain in the released material. Its evidentiary function on this board is different and arguably stronger: it is a fully documented instance of a state deciding that objects it could not identify were interfering with its citizens, tasking a named officer to go and look, and then, three decades on, handing the whole file to the reading public. Every element of that sequence has a missing US counterpart, and the absence of the counterpart is the finding.

### What it has denied

The operation's own final report, signed by its commanding officer, is a negative finding: no signs of extraterrestrials at Colares. That report is the denial, and it is exactly the kind of denial this board catalogues rather than accepts, because it is dated, signed and attributable, and because the signatory publicly repudiated it twenty years later before dying by suicide within weeks. The FAB has never withdrawn or reaffirmed the report; it has simply released the file.

Roles the board assigns it: research, custody, disclosure, concealment.

### Open questions on this node

- The precise phase dates of the operation vary by source. One commonly cited framing gives two phases, 20 October to 11 November 1977 and 25 November to 5 December 1977, while other accounts extend the operation into 1978 and the archive compilation covers 2 September 1977 to 28 November 1978. No primary FAB order fixing the phases has been retrieved.
- The identity and archival citation of Hollanda's signed final report inside BR DFANBSB ARX have not been pinned to a specific SIAN item number in this pass.
- The attribution of the ARX 184 compilation to a report signed by Brigadeiro Jose Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque rests on a secondary blog account and has not been verified against the archive item.
- Whether the Colares hospital admissions were medically documented in the military file, and whether those medical records were transferred with the rest, is unresolved.
- The circumstances of Hollanda's death are recorded as suicide and the principal interviewer rejects foul play. The board should not treat the sequence of recantation followed by death as evidentiary without a document that has not been produced.
- No released Operacao Prato material speaks to recovery of material, custody of hardware, or reverse-engineering.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 70 of 1057, score 70). 10 connections, 5 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A federal suit to compel declassification of Operação Prato films (Rafael Zatarian Pederneiras v. União) was dismissed and the dismissal upheld on 27 February 2023. The court held that the claim of surviving FAB UFO films 'tem lastro especialmente em matérias jornalísticas e relatos de testemunhas, de modo que não constituem indícios suficientes quanto à existência de tais arquivos', and applied the presumption of veracity to the Administration's statement that the surviving records are no longer classified and are held by the Arquivo Nacional. Source: Justiça Federal SC, Recurso Cível n. 5002417-48.2022.4.04.7200/SC, RELVOTO, 27/2/2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** Archival unit designation attested in a parliamentary document: the summary of SINDACTA I UFO cases sent to COMDABRA is recorded 'no Envelope nº. 10 da década de 1970'. The related petition is Processo nº 60.000.000026/2008-45 ('Dossiê UFO BRASIL'), filed at the Ministério da Defesa on 2 January 2008; the Operação Prato film material is its item 'j'. Source: Câmara dos Deputados, RIC 679/2011, items 1.1 and 1.2, 22 June 2011
- **Tier 1 documented.** Arquivo Nacional's own response to a litigant, quoted verbatim in the federal court record: 'Em pesquisa no SIAN, usando o termo "operação prato", retornam 6 (seis) documentos. Em nenhum deles há documentos de caráter audiovisual.' The catalogue therefore yields six items under that search term and no audiovisual material. NO ITEM-LEVEL SIAN REFERENCE CODES FOR THESE SIX WERE OBTAINABLE - see 'unresolved'. ## Noite Oficial dos OVNIs - the Brazilian 'Night of the UFOs', 19 May 1986

Noite Oficial dos OVNIs - the Brazilian 'Night of the UFOs', 19 May 1986 is a event on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1986-05-19 to 1986-05-20; axes C1 A2 D3; 6 connections; in the research band.

In one line: 19-20 May 1986; 21 radar plots, five FAB fighters scrambled from Santa Cruz (RJ) and Anápolis (GO) across four Brazilian states; ministerial press conference 23 May 1986; report released 25 September 2009.

### What it is

The 'Noite Oficial dos OVNIs', the Official Night of the UFOs, is the night of 19 May 1986, when Brazilian civil and military radar tracked multiple unidentified returns across several states in the country's south-east and centre-west and the Forca Aerea Brasileira launched interceptors against them. Five fighters, a mix of F-5E and Mirage aircraft, were scrambled from Santa Cruz air base in Sao Paulo state and Anapolis air base in Goias. Four days later the Minister of Aeronautics, Brigadeiro Octavio Julio Moreira Lima, held a press conference at the ministry in Brasilia, presented controllers and pilots involved, and said the air force had no technical explanation.

### Why it is implicated

This is the single most important comparison point on the board, because it is the event the United States has never had and, on the concealment thesis, cannot have. A cabinet-rank defence official stood at a podium four days after a multi-aircraft intercept mission against radar-confirmed unidentified objects, named the mission, produced the aircrew and the controllers, and stated on the record that the state could not explain what it had chased. Nothing about Brazil's position in 1986 made this cheap: the country was two years out of military rule, the air force ministry was a serving-officer institution, and the radar and intercept performance data at issue were exactly the sort of material the US cites as the reason such events cannot be discussed. The 1986 night therefore converts the US argument from a claim about the sensitivity of the phenomenon into a claim about the sensitivity of the institution. It also carries its own lag, which is the honest half of the story: the minister promised a written document within thirty days and the report did not reach the public until September 2009, twenty-three years later.

### The longer account

The evening of 19 May 1986 produced a cascade rather than a single sighting. Radar returns began appearing on civil scopes serving Sao Paulo airports and on the military picture held by CINDACTA, the Integrated Air Defence and Air Traffic Control Centre, and the objects were also acquired visually from the air. Among the airborne witnesses was Ozires Silva, at the time president of Petrobras and one of the most consequential figures in Brazilian aviation as the founder of Embraer, who was flying in an executive Xingu and who, with his pilot, pursued lights for roughly twenty-five minutes. The reported speeds spanned a wide band, from a few hundred kilometres per hour to well over a thousand, which is itself a characteristic of the case: the objects were not behaving like a single misidentified class of target.

The air force treated it as an air defence problem. Five interceptors went up, two F-5E from Santa Cruz in Sao Paulo state and three Mirage from Anapolis in Goias. Pilots reported acquisitions and losses, and the objects did not resolve into anything the crews could identify. In the Brazilian Senate's own later account of the night, twenty-one objects were involved and were pursued for hours.

What makes the date load-bearing is 23 May, not 19 May. The scale of the press coverage forced a response, and the response was a press conference at the Ministry of Aeronautics in Brasilia at which Moreira Lima appeared with air traffic controllers and air force pilots from the mission. He confirmed that five FAB fighters had pursued unidentified objects over Brazilian territory and, asked to account for them, said that technically he would tell them there was no explanation. Contemporary and retrospective Brazilian reporting has treated the phrase as the moment the state conceded, in public, at ministerial level, that its air defence system had engaged something it could not name. Documents later associated with the case describe the objects as solid bodies displaying intelligent behaviour, including formation flight and the ability to track aircraft.

The US comparison is not close. The nearest American analogue in kind, the Washington DC overflights of July 1952, produced a press conference that went the opposite way: the Air Force convened the largest such briefing since the Second World War in order to deliver an explanation, temperature inversion, and to close the matter. In 1986 Brasilia did not reach for an explanation because it did not have one, and said so. Nothing in the subsequent four decades suggests the admission harmed Brazilian air defence, deterrence, or public order. That absence of consequence is the evidence.

The concealment element sits in the timeline afterwards. The minister undertook to produce a written document on the incident and to release it within thirty days. That did not happen. The report became public only in September 2009, which places it inside the same window as the FAB's initial transfers to the Arquivo Nacional and just ahead of Portaria 551/GC3 in August 2010. So the correct reading of the 1986 night is two-part. The verbal disclosure was immediate, unqualified and at cabinet level. The documentary disclosure took twenty-three years and arrived only once the Aeronautica had built an archival pipeline that made release the default. A board tracking concealment should treat that gap as the ordinary metabolism of a defence bureaucracy, and should note that even a system with a twenty-three year documentary lag still ended in publication, which the US system has not.

Today the material sits in the Arquivo Nacional's Fundo OVNI. The archive itself flags 1986 as one of the best known cases in the collection, with around twenty-one unidentified objects detected on 19 May, and the fund includes audio recordings of exchanges between pilots and control tower operators alongside the written reports. In May and June 2026 the fortieth anniversary drew substantial Brazilian press coverage, including detailed reconstructions in the military press, which is a measure of how thoroughly the event has become part of the country's public record rather than its classified one. The comparison the board is built to make is therefore not rhetorical. It is a specific, dated, documented sequence in one country against a specific, dated, documented absence in another.

### What it has denied

There is no denial to catalogue for 19 May 1986, and that absence is the point of the node. The Minister of Aeronautics affirmed the intercept mission at a press conference four days later and stated that the air force had no technical explanation, which is the opposite of the American pattern of supplying an explanation in order to close a case. The only concealment behaviour attaching to this event is procedural delay: a written report promised within thirty days that did not become public until September 2009.

Roles the board assigns it: research, disclosure, concealment, custody.

### Open questions on this node

- No verbatim transcript of the 23 May 1986 press conference has been located in a primary Brazilian government source in this pass; the minister's most quoted sentence rests on Brazilian press reporting.
- The object count varies. The Arquivo Nacional and the Senate record both use 21; some English-language accounts say 'as many as twenty'. The underlying FAB tally has not been checked against the archive item.
- The specific SIAN item number for the September 2009 report on the 1986 events has not been identified.
- The names of the intercept pilots and the tail numbers of the five aircraft have not been confirmed against a primary FAB source.
- Whether any radar tape, gun-camera film or onboard sensor data from the intercepts was retained, and whether it was included in the transfer to the Arquivo Nacional, is unresolved.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 125 of 1057, score 55). 6 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The 1986 event is named and characterised in a Senate document approved by the Plenary on 24 June 2022: 'em 1986, quando ocorreu a hoje conhecida como "Noite Oficial dos UFOs no Brasil", quando 21 OVNIs esféricos de 100 metros de diâmetro cada foram perseguidos durante horas por nossos caças a jato.' This attests the legislative record of the event, not the physical claims within it. Source: Senado Federal, RQS 193/2022, justificação; Diário do Senado Federal nº 32 de 2022; approved 24/06/2022
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Air Defence Command's report records the interceptor sequence with Zulu timings, the 13 plots astern of the second Santa Cruz F-5, and the abandonment of pursuits at bingo fuel and on loss of contact. Source: Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, Relatório de Ocorrências, 2 June 1986, Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional identifies the 1986 'Noite Oficial dos OVNIs' of 19 May, with around 21 unidentified flying objects detected, as one of the most famous cases documented in its OVNI collection. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Brazilian Senate's official record describes the 1986 event as the 'Noite Oficial dos UFOs no Brasil', in which 21 spherical objects, each stated at 100 metres in diameter, were pursued for hours by Brazilian jet fighters. Source: Senado Federal, Requerimento no 193, de 2022, justificacao, presented 16 March 2022, https://legis.senado.leg.br/sdleg-getter/documento?dm=9089831&disposition=inline
- **Tier 1 documented.** The report records the technical limitation that Brazilian coverage rested on radars 'cuja finalidade primária é destinado ao controle de tráfego aéreo, não sendo portanto, específico para emprego em Defesa Aérea', making surveillance deficient. Source: Relatório de Ocorrências, 2 June 1986, Considerações Iniciais
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** A month after the event two officers of the Aerospace Technical Centre (CTA), accompanied by the American researcher James J. Hurtak, collected the photographic negatives taken by press photographer Adenir Britto from the newspaper Vale Paraibano, saying they would be analysed by NASA; they were never returned. Source: BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022, interview with Adenir Britto
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Captain Viriato, TV Globo 'Globo Repórter' interview, 1993, on the object that accelerated away from his Mirage at Mach 1.3: 'Se existe avião que possa desenvolver essa velocidade, eu desconheço.' Source: BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022, quoting the 1993 Globo Repórter interview
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Civil-aviation corroboration on the night: at 21h04 the São José dos Campos controller Sargento Sérgio Mota da Silva contacted the twin-engine Xingu, prefix PT-MBZ, returning from Brasília, carrying Coronel Ozires Silva (who took office as president of Petrobras the following day) and copilot Alcir Pereira da Silva. Sightings were also reported by a TAM Bandeirante on the Londrina-São Paulo route and a Transbrasil aircraft over Araxá (MG). Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022 / 17 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Documents and audio recordings relating to the events of 19 May 1986 were subsequently released through the Arquivo Nacional, whose OVNI fund includes audio of exchanges between pilots and control tower operators. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Four days after the event, on 23 May 1986, the Minister of Aeronautics, Brigadeiro Octavio Julio Moreira Lima, convened a press conference at which he confirmed that five FAB fighters had pursued unidentified flying objects over Brazilian territory. Source: Revista Sociedade Militar, 'Ha 40 anos, cacas da FAB perseguiram objetos misteriosos no ceu brasileiro em episodio ainda cercado de enigmas', 2026, https://www.sociedademilitar.com.br/2026/05/noite-dos-ovnis-ha-40-anos-cacas-da-fab-perseguiram-objetos-nos-ceus-brasileiros-em-episodio-ainda-cercado-de-misterios-fbnc.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Full scramble order of battle, pilots, aircraft prefixes and bases: (1) F-5, prefix FAB-4848, Tenente Kleber Caldas Marinho, 22h34, from Base Aérea de Santa Cruz (RJ); (2) Mirage F-103, prefix 4913, Capitão Armindo Sousa Viriato de Freitas, 22h48, from Base Aérea de Anápolis (GO); (3) F-5, prefix FAB-4849, Capitão Márcio Brisolla Jordão, 22h50, from Santa Cruz (RJ); (4) Mirage, prefix FAB-4918, Capitão Rodolfo Silva e Souza, 23h17, Anápolis; (5) Mirage, prefix FAB-4917, Capitão Júlio Cézar Rozemberg, 23h46, Anápolis. The last two had no visual or radar contact. All three initial aircraft were given the same tasking: 'interceptação não agressiva'. Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022 / 17 May 2026. NOTE: prefix FAB-4913 for the Viriato Mirage and the 22h48 (not 22h50) departure time differ in detail from earlier board entries; Tier 1 confirmation requires the Relatório de Ocorrências itself in SIAN, which could not be machine-retrieved.
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Ministerial press conference record: on 23 May 1986 at 16h30 the Minister of Aeronautics, Brigadeiro Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima (1926-2011), convened the press to state that five FAB fighters had pursued 21 UFOs. His words as reported: 'Não se trata de acreditar ou não [em seres extraterrestres ou em discos voadores]. Só podemos dar informações técnicas. As suposições são várias. Tecnicamente, diria aos senhores que não temos explicação.' The five FAB pilots and the on-duty flight controllers attended. Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 'A noite em que 21 óvnis invadiram o espaço aéreo brasileiro e foram perseguidos por caças da FAB', published 14 January 2022, republished 17 May 2026 (https://www.opovo.com.br/agencia/bbc/2026/05/17/amp/a-noite-em-que-21-ovnis-invadiram-o-espaco-aereo-brasileiro-e-foram-perseguidos-por-cacas-da-fab.html)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the night of 19 May 1986 multiple unidentified objects were tracked over several Brazilian states and the Forca Aerea Brasileira scrambled five supersonic fighters, F-5E and Mirage aircraft, in an attempt at interception. Source: O Tempo, 'Noite Oficial dos Ovnis: caso que mobilizou cacas da FAB completa 40 anos e ainda intriga o Brasil', 16 May 2026, https://www.otempo.com.br/brasil/2026/5/16/noite-oficial-dos-ovnis-caso-que-mobilizou-cacas-da-fab-completa-40-anos-e-ainda-intriga-o-brasil
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Pilot and aircraft identifications: Lt Kleber Caldas Marinho (F-5, FAB-4848, 22:34 from Santa Cruz); Capt Márcio Brisolla Jordão (F-5, FAB-4849, 22:50); Capt Armindo Sousa Viriato de Freitas (Mirage, Anápolis); Capt Rodolfo Silva e Souza (FAB-4918, 23:17); Capt Júlio Cézar Rozemberg (FAB-4917, 23:46). Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The 23-year delay: at the close of the 23 May 1986 press conference the Minister undertook to publish a complete dossier 'dentro de 30 dias'. The report - signed by the interim Comando da Aeronáutica (COMDA) commander José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque and dated 2 June 1986 - was released only on 25 September 2009, 23 years later. Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022 / 17 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The FAB's standing position, restated in writing in August 2026, is that all Comando da Aeronautica material on unidentified aerial phenomena from 1952 to 2025, which includes the 1986 file, has been transferred to the Arquivo Nacional and is in the public domain. Source: Nota da Forca Aerea reproduced in CNN Brasil, 12 August 2026, https://www.cnnbrasil.com.br/ciencia/pilotos-e-controladores-relataram-18-casos-de-ovnis-no-brasil-em-2025/

# 2000-2016: AAWSAP / AATIP

## Office of the Director of National Intelligence

Office of the Director of National Intelligence is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2005-04-22; axes C1 A3 D3; 18 connections; in the research band.

In one line: Head of the 18-element Intelligence Community; co-signatory of the UAP reporting regime.

### What it is

The Office of the Director of National Intelligence is the coordinating headquarters of the US Intelligence Community, created by the Intelligence Reform and Terrorism Prevention Act of 2004 and headed by the Director of National Intelligence. Since 2021 it has been the statutory co-owner of the government's UAP reporting machinery: it published the June 2021 'Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena,' it is the office with which the Secretary of Defense must coordinate in establishing and running AARO, and it co-issues the annual consolidated UAP reports. Its Inspector General is also the channel through which UAP whistleblower complaints reach the congressional intelligence committees.

### Why it is implicated

ODNI sits on both sides of the ledger, and that is precisely what makes it useful. It is the office that publicly certified that most reported UAP 'probably do represent physical objects,' that eleven documented pilot near-misses exist, and, critically, that it could not confirm that any of the collected reports were attributable to classified US government or industry developmental programs. An intelligence headquarters that cannot deconflict a UAP dataset against its own black programs is either not being told, or is telling us it is not being told. At the same time, ODNI is the institutional counterparty for every DoD SAP that shares compartmented capabilities with the IC, and AARO is lodged inside the DoD office designated as ODNI's primary interface. Its Inspector General found David Grusch's complaint alleging concealed retrieval and reverse-engineering programs 'credible and urgent' and forwarded it to the DNI and both intelligence committees, after which nothing publicly followed. Even the FOIA-released version of its own 2021 assessment is redacted under (b)(1) and (b)(3), including the identity of the official it names as accountable.

### The longer account

ODNI's formal entry into the UAP file came through an appropriations rider rather than through the intelligence community's own initiative. Senate Report 116-233, accompanying the Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2021, directed the DNI, in consultation with the Secretary of Defense, to submit an intelligence assessment of the threat posed by unidentified aerial phenomena and of the progress the Department of Defense Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force had made in understanding it. The resulting nine-page unclassified 'Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena,' dated 25 June 2021, is the single most-cited government document on the subject, and it repays close reading of the FOIA copy rather than the sanitised public one.

The drafting roster alone is analytically valuable. The report was 'drafted by the UAPTF and the ODNI National Intelligence Manager for Aviation, with input from USD(I&S), DIA, FBI, NRO, NGA, NSA, Air Force, Army, Navy, Marine Corps, DARPA, FAA, NASA, NOAA, ODNI/NIM-Emerging and Disruptive Technology, ODNI/National Counterintelligence and Security Center, and ODNI/National Intelligence Council.' That is a list of every organisation with an equity in the file as of 2021, and it is a map of where records exist.

The substantive findings are hedged but not empty. 'Most of the UAP reported probably do represent physical objects given that a majority of UAP registered across multiple sensors, to include radar, infrared, electro-optical, weapon seekers, and visual observation.' The UAPTF 'has 11 reports of documented instances in which pilots reported near misses with a UAP.' The dataset is limited primarily to US government reporting of incidents from November 2004 to March 2021. The report proposes five explanatory bins, airborne clutter, natural atmospheric phenomena, USG or US industry developmental programs, foreign adversary systems, and a catchall 'other', and then, on the third bin, delivers the sentence a program hypothesis must build on: 'Some UAP observations could be attributable to classified USG aerospace programs or systems under development by commercial aerospace firms. We were unable to confirm, however, that these systems accounted for any of the UAP reports we collected.' On foreign adversaries: 'We currently lack data to determine any UAP are part of a foreign collection program or indicative of a major technological advancement by a potential adversary.' On collection: 'The UAPTF had no MASINT reporting on the events considered in this dataset', which stands in direct tension with the later Immaculate Constellation allegation that a parent USAP holds high-quality IMINT and MASINT of UAP.

The redaction pattern is itself evidence. The FOIA version, approved for release on 24 March 2022 under case DF-2021-00275, still withholds material under (b)(1), properly classified national defence information, and (b)(3), statutory exemption. The sentence identifying the accountable official reads, in the released copy, 'The UAPTF Director, is the official accountable for ensuring the timely collection and consolidation of data on UAP,' with the name removed. Passages describing unusual flight characteristics, GEOINT and SIGINT collection, and specific incidents are blank. The unclassified public product is therefore a curated fraction of what Congress received, and the delta is where the argument lives.

ODNI's second role is structural. Under 50 U.S.C. 3373 the Secretary of Defense must act 'in coordination with the Director of National Intelligence' in establishing AARO, and the reporting mechanism must be established 'in consultation with the Director of National Intelligence.' AARO is housed inside OUSD(I&S). And DoDD 5205.07, effective 12 September 2024 and approved by Deputy Secretary of Defense Kathleen Hicks, designates USD(I&S) as the office that 'serves as the primary interface for the Office of the Director of National Intelligence (ODNI) and members of the Intelligence Community (IC), including sharing with the DoD compartmented capabilities and information intended for inclusion and subsequent apportionment.' In other words, the same office that hosts the body Congress created to find unreported UAP programs is the office through which IC compartmented capabilities are moved into and out of DoD special access programs. Whether that is an oversight design flaw or the point is the question the board exists to press.

The third role is the whistleblower channel. In 2022, while at the National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency, David Grusch filed a Presidential Policy Directive 19 urgent-concern complaint with the Intelligence Community Inspector General. Thomas Monheim, IC Inspector General from April 2020 to January 2025, found the complaint credible and urgent, and a summary went to the Director of National Intelligence and to the House and Senate intelligence committees. Grusch then testified publicly to House Oversight in July 2023. No ODNI product has since publicly addressed the substance of the referral.

Finally, ODNI has been the co-publisher of record for the successor reporting. The Fiscal Year 2024 Consolidated Annual Report on Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena, released 14 November 2024 the day after the House Oversight hearing, reported 757 UAP reports received, 485 of which occurred within the reporting period and 272 of which occurred in 2021-2022 but were reported late; 118 cases resolved to prosaic objects; 21 unresolved. That report is a DoD/AARO product carried on the ODNI website, and under this board's sourcing rules it is a primary record of what the government said, not of what is true.

What the record does not show is any ODNI statement about retrievals, materials, or reverse-engineering. There is no ODNI acknowledgement of a legacy program, and no public ODNI response to the Grusch referral.

### What it has denied

ODNI has never denied that a legacy retrieval or reverse-engineering program exists; it has denied confirmation. Its 2021 assessment states it was 'unable to confirm' that US classified aerospace programs accounted for any collected UAP report, and that it 'currently lacks data' to attribute UAP to a foreign collection program. The characteristic ODNI posture is epistemic hedging plus redaction, not categorical denial, which is a materially different artifact from the flat DoD denials on this board.

Roles the board assigns it: oversight, concealment, disclosure, research.

### Open questions on this node

- Who is the UAPTF Director redacted under (b)(6) in the FOIA copy of the 2021 assessment?
- What does the classified annex to the 2021 preliminary assessment contain, and who at ODNI has seen it?
- What action, if any, did ODNI or the DNI take on the ICIG's credible-and-urgent referral of the Grusch complaint?
- Has ODNI or any IC element transferred records to NARA Record Group 615, and what did they withhold?
- Which ODNI element holds equity in any UAP-related compartmented capability shared with DoD SAPs through the USD(I&S) interface?
- Why could the IC not deconflict its own UAP dataset against classified US aerospace programs, was the deconfliction attempted and refused, or never possible at the UAPTF's access level?

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 120 of 1057, score 55). 18 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD Directive 5205.07, effective 12 September 2024 and approved by Deputy Secretary of Defense Kathleen H. Hicks, designates USD(I&S) as the office that 'serves as the primary interface for the Office of the Director of National Intelligence (ODNI) and members of the Intelligence Community (IC), including sharing with the DoD compartmented capabilities and information intended for inclusion and subsequent apportionment', the same office in which AARO is housed. Source: https://www.esd.whs.mil/portals/54/documents/dd/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** ODNI is one of four agencies (with OSD, FAA and the Nuclear Regulatory Commission) whose UAP records NARA announced on 24 April 2025 had been transferred into the statutory UAP Collection under sections 1841-1843 of Public Law 118-31. Source: National Archives press release nr25-07, 24 April 2025
- **Tier 1 documented.** ODNI published 'Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena' on 25 June 2021, analysing 144 reports from USG sources of which 80 involved multiple sensors, sorted into five explanatory categories. Source: ODNI Preliminary Assessment, 25 June 2021
- **Tier 1 documented.** On the possibility that UAP are US black programs the assessment states: 'Some UAP observations could be attributable to classified USG aerospace programs or systems under development by commercial aerospace firms. We were unable to confirm, however, that these systems accounted for any of the UAP reports we collected.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sections 1841-1843 of the FY2024 NDAA, now codified at 44 U.S.C. 2107 note, require NARA to establish the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection consisting of 'copies of all Government, Government-provided, or Government-funded records relating to unidentified anomalous phenomena, technologies of unknown origin, and non-human intelligence'; NARA established Record Group 615 for it, creating a statutory transfer obligation that reaches every IC element ODNI coordinates. Source: https://www.archives.gov/research/topics/uaps/faqs
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 2021 Preliminary Assessment names as contributors USD(I&S), DIA, FBI, NRO, NGA, NSA, Air Force, Army, Navy/ONI, DARPA, FAA, NOAA, ODNI/NIM-Emerging and Disruptive Technology, ODNI/NCSC and the National Intelligence Council. Source: ODNI Preliminary Assessment, 25 June 2021
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 25 June 2021 'Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena' was produced by ODNI in response to a provision in Senate Report 116-233 accompanying the Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2021, directing the DNI in consultation with the Secretary of Defense to assess the threat posed by UAP and the UAPTF's progress. Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The FOIA-released version of the assessment, approved for release by ODNI on 24 March 2022 under case DF-2021-00275, withholds material under exemptions (b)(1) and (b)(3), including the name of the UAPTF Director identified in the text as 'the official accountable for ensuring the timely collection and consolidation of data on UAP,' redacted under (b)(3) and (b)(6). Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Preliminary Assessment states that 'The limited amount of high-quality reporting on unidentified aerial phenomena (UAP) hampers our ability to draw firm conclusions about the nature or intent of UAP.' Source: ODNI Preliminary Assessment, 25 June 2021
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment defines five explanatory categories into which resolved UAP incidents would fall: airborne clutter, natural atmospheric phenomena, USG or U.S. industry developmental programs, foreign adversary systems, and a catchall 'other' bin. Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment notes 'some UAP have been detected near military facilities or by aircraft carrying the USG's most advanced sensor systems.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment states it 'was drafted by the UAPTF and the ODNI National Intelligence Manager for Aviation, with input from USD(I&S), DIA, FBI, NRO, NGA, NSA, Air Force, Army, Navy, Marine Corps, DARPA, FAA, NASA, NOAA, ODNI/NIM-Emerging and Disruptive Technology, ODNI/National Counterintelligence and Security Center, and ODNI/National Intelligence Council.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment states: 'Most of the UAP reported probably do represent physical objects given that a majority of UAP registered across multiple sensors, to include radar, infrared, electro-optical, weapon seekers, and visual observation.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment states: 'The UAPTF had no MASINT reporting on the events considered in this dataset.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The assessment states: 'The UAPTF has 11 reports of documented instances in which pilots reported near misses with a UAP.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The dataset described in the assessment 'is currently limited primarily to USG reporting of incidents occurring from November 2004 to March 2021.' Source: https://archive.dni.gov/files/documents/FOIA/DF-2021-00275-Preliminary-Assessment-Unidentified-Aerial-Phenomena.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 50 U.S.C. 3373 requires the Secretary of Defense, in coordination with the Director of National Intelligence, to establish the office known as the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, and requires the secure UAP reporting mechanism to be established in consultation with the DNI. Source: https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/50/3373
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In 2022 Intelligence Community Inspector General Thomas Monheim found David Grusch's PPD-19 urgent-concern complaint credible and urgent, and a summary was transmitted to the Director of National Intelligence and to the House and Senate intelligence committees; Grusch subsequently testified publicly to House Oversight in July 2023. Source: https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/Dave_G_HOC_Speech_FINAL_For_Trans.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Michael Shellenberger's November 2024 congressional testimony quotes DNI John Ratcliffe stating in 2021 that UAP demonstrated 'technologies that we don't have and, frankly, that we are not capable of defending against,' that analysts had 'high confidence' foreign adversaries were not behind the Tic Tac encounter, and that 'there are a lot more sightings than have been made public.' Source: https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Written-Testimony-Shellenberger.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The Fiscal Year 2024 Consolidated Annual Report on Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena, released 14 November 2024 and carried on the ODNI website, reports 757 UAP reports received, of which 485 occurred within the reporting period and 272 occurred in 2021-2022 but were reported late; 118 cases were resolved to prosaic objects and 21 remain unexplained. Source: https://media.defense.gov/2024/Nov/14/2003583603/-1/-1/0/FY24-CONSOLIDATED-ANNUAL-REPORT-ON-UAP-508.PDF

## GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés

GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2005; axes C1 A1 D3; 10 connections; in the research band.

In one line: CNES unit, Toulouse; the world's only permanent civilian state UAP office with a public case database.

### What it is

France has run a state UAP office inside its space agency continuously since 1977 and publishes the case files

### Why it is implicated

GEIPAN is the answer to the question the US posture keeps deflecting: what does it actually look like when a government decides that unidentified aerospace phenomena are a normal object of public administration. The answer is a two- or three-person office in a space agency, a volunteer investigator corps, a steering committee of police, gendarmes, air force, meteorologists and scientists, a published four-tier classification, and a searchable public archive of every closed case. It has run without interruption since 1977 under three names.

### The longer account

GEIPAN is the answer to the question the US posture keeps deflecting: what does it actually look like when a government decides that unidentified aerospace phenomena are a normal object of public administration. The answer is a two- or three-person office in a space agency, a volunteer investigator corps, a steering committee of police, gendarmes, air force, meteorologists and scientists, a published four-tier classification, and a searchable public archive of every closed case. It has run without interruption since 1977 under three names.

CNES states plainly on its own site that it has studied unidentified aerospace phenomena since 1977, the year GEPAN - the Groupe d'Étude des Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés - was created. Its origin was not a scandal or a leak but a recommendation: a 1976 Institut des Hautes Études de Défense Nationale committee, chaired by General Blanchard of the Gendarmerie Nationale, opened the UFO file with the explicit objective of making proposals for organising research and data collection, and GEPAN was created on the back of that committee's recommendations. That lineage is stated in the COMETA report's own introduction. Claude Poher directed GEPAN at its inception. In 1988 GEPAN was replaced by SEPRA, the Service d'Études des Phénomènes de Rentrées Atmosphériques, which absorbed the UAP function into a unit whose principal job was routine monitoring of orbital debris and re-entry risk objects. At the end of 2001 the CNES Direction Générale commissioned an audit to decide the activity's future. The 2005 outcome was restructuring rather than closure: GEIPAN replaced SEPRA, and the audit carried what CNES calls 'une recommandation importante d'informer le public en toute transparence' - an important recommendation to inform the public in complete transparency. That recommendation is embedded in the acronym; the added 'I' stands for Information. It was implemented starting in 2007 with the online publication of GEIPAN's archives and case files.

The structure is worth stating precisely because it is the operational counter-model. GEIPAN sits under the deputy directorate of the Toulouse Space Centre. It is steered by a comité de pilotage (COPEIPAN) chaired by a recognised figure from the aerospace world and composed of representatives of the country's civil and military authorities - Gendarmerie nationale, civil aviation, meteorology, the Air and Space Force, scientific research - plus CNES. That committee's job is to analyse GEIPAN's results and make recommendations to CNES on its direction. Beneath it sits a collège d'experts of roughly thirty multidisciplinary volunteers who take the hardest cases, and a corps of volunteer field investigators across France who are mandated case by case and work to the methods set out in GEIPAN's investigator guide. GEIPAN holds standing conventions with the Gendarmerie, the Air Force, civil aviation, the Navy and Météo France for rapid access to hard data, and works with CNRS and the CEA. The permanent staff, per GEIPAN's own history page, is two full-time CNES agents - the head and an assistant - supported by external contractors for case analysis, statistics, anonymisation and archiving. Canada's Office of the Chief Science Advisor, surveying the office in 2025, counted three full-time employees and over 20 trained volunteer investigators. Funding comes out of the general public-service subsidy CNES receives; there is no separate UAP line item. Frederic Courtade has headed GEIPAN since January 2024.

The classification is what makes the archive usable. Each case is scored on two axes - consistency (C), the product of the quantity and reliability of data, and residual strangeness (E) after comparison against known-phenomenon hypotheses - then filed as A (perfectly identified), B (probably identified), C (unidentified for lack of data) or D (unidentified after investigation). Cases in C and D can be reopened if new information arrives. As of the site statistics dated 11 August 2026, 3,381 published classified cases break down as 950 class A (28.1%), 1,310 class B (38.7%), 1,015 class C (30.0%) and 106 class D (3.1%). Canada's report, working from the 2025 figures, recorded 3,188 cases with 3.2% unidentified after investigation and 31.4% unexplained for insufficient data. The residual is small, it is stable, and it is public.

GEIPAN is also unusually explicit about what it is not. Its FAQ states that it is not an independent structure but a technical department of CNES; that it carries no national-security, space-surveillance or alert mission; and that, 'contrary to popular belief', it is not a research group on UFOs, extraterrestrial life or other paranormal phenomena. It uses only recognised scientific knowledge, 'without speculation or unverified hypotheses'. That disclaimer is not evasion - it is the price of the archive, and it is precisely the trade the US system has refused to make.

The substantive work is on the record. In June 1978 GEPAN presented its scientific council with a five-volume, roughly 500-page report on the first semester of 1978; a contemporaneous English-language summary of that report, held in the NSA's public UFO document collection, records that eleven high-credibility, high-strangeness cases were studied in detail, that only one proved to have a conventional explanation, that in ten of the eleven the conclusion was that witnesses had seen a material phenomenon explicable as neither a natural phenomenon nor a human device, and that the report concluded there was behind the phenomenon a 'flying machine. . . whose modes of sustenance and propulsion are beyond our knowledge'. The same summary judges that the care taken with distances, angles and psychological factors 'makes the bulk of the Condon Report seem very poor by comparison'. GEPAN's most cited physical-trace investigation, the Trans-en-Provence landing of 8 January 1981, was published as Note Technique n° 16, 'Analyse d'une trace', issued by CNES in Toulouse on 1 March 1983 and still hosted on the GEIPAN site.

The office's standing outside France is the argument in compressed form. When Canada's Chief Science Advisor set out to design a national UAP reporting system in 2025, GEIPAN was the model it described in the most detail, the example it explicitly told Canada to consider when establishing a dedicated service, and one of only four international bodies - alongside AARO, NASA and Chile's SEFAA - that it recommended Canada partner with.

Roles the board assigns it: research, disclosure.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 81 of 1057, score 68). 10 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Au total le GEIPAN reçoit plus de 1000 contacts par an ... Environ 150 à 200 donnent lieu à une enquête qui se termine par une publication sur le site du GEIPAN ... tout en préservant l'anonymat des témoins.' Source: GEIPAN, 'Classification', geipan.fr, 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Depuis 49 ans, le GEIPAN a enregistré plus de 9 897 témoignages représentant environ 5 170 cas d'observations qui dans 10% des cas ont fait objet d'enquêtes terrain. Au 31/03/2026 cela représente 3336 cas publiés. 66,5 % des cas sont classés A ou B ... 30.3 % ... (CAS C). 3,2 % des observations restent inexpliquées (CAS D).' Source: GEIPAN, 'Classification', geipan.fr, statistics as at 31 March 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Fin 2001, la Direction générale du CNES commande un audit pour statuer sur l'avenir de l'activité. En 2005, à la suite d'un audit, le Président du CNES décide la restructuration de l'activité et le GEIPAN ... se substitue au SEPRA, avec une recommandation importante d'informer le public en toute transparence.' The 'I' in GEIPAN stands for Information, and the recommendation was implemented through publication of the archives 'entamée en 2007'. Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'L'équipe du GEIPAN est constituée de deux agents CNES à temps plein: le responsable et son assistante', with external support for case study, statistics, anonymisation and archiving; its budget comes from the general public-service subsidy received by CNES rather than a dedicated allocation. Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Le CNES étudie les PAN depuis 1977, date de la création du GEPAN (Groupe d'Étude des Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés). En 1988, le GEPAN est remplacé par le SEPRA (Service d'Études des Phénomènes de Rentrées Atmosphériques).' Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** CNES lists GEIPAN's inception as 1977, its location as CNES Toulouse, its lifetime as 'Indefinite' and its status as 'In operation', with partners Gendarmerie, Police, Air and Space Force, CNRS and Meteo France; 'Currently headed by Frédéric Courtade since January 2024, GEIPAN is overseen by COPEIPAN, a steering committee'. Source: https://cnes.fr/en/projects/geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** Canada's Office of the Chief Science Advisor: 'France's GEIPAN ... operates under the French space agency CNES ... since 1977. Its mission is to collect, investigate and archive UAP reports, and make its findings available to the public.' It records 'of the 3,188 cases collected by 2025, only 3.2% of cases remained unidentified after investigation. Furthermore, 31.4% of cases could not be explained due to insufficient data.' Source: Office of the Chief Science Advisor of Canada, 'Management of Public Reporting of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in Canada', 2025 - https://ised-isde.canada.ca/site/science/en/office-chief-science-advisor/sky-canada-project/management-public-reporting-unidentified-aerial-phenomena-canada
- **Tier 1 documented.** Classification is by consistency (C) and residual strangeness (E), producing categories A (perfectly identified), B (probably identified), C (unidentified due to lack of data) and D (unidentified after investigation); C and D cases may be revisited if new information is supplied. Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEIPAN announced 'Un nouveau responsable au GEIPAN à compter du 15 janvier 2024' on 12 February 2024. Source: GEIPAN news item, geipan.fr, 12 February 2024
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEIPAN dynamic statistics as at 11 August 2026: 3,381 cases total - A 28.1% (950), B 38.7% (1,310), C 30.0% (1,015), D 3.1% (106). Source: GEIPAN, 'Statistiques', geipan.fr, 11 August 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEIPAN is steered by a comité de pilotage 'composé de représentants des autorités civiles et militaires du pays (Gendarmerie nationale, Aviation civile, Météo, Armée de l'air, Recherche scientifique) et du CNES', supported by a collège d'experts of 'une trentaine de bénévoles' and volunteer field investigators across France. Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEIPAN uses IPACO as its preferred image and video authentication and processing software for investigations (news item, 20 May 2025). Source: GEIPAN, 'IPACO: Le logiciel d'authentification et de traitement d'images du GEIPAN', geipan.fr, 20 May 2025
- **Tier 1 documented, allegation shaped wording as stored.** GEIPAN's FAQ states it is 'a technical department of CNES', that it 'does not involve in a security topic nor a sky and space awareness or alert service', and that 'contrary to popular belief, GEIPAN is not a research group on UFOs and extraterrestrial life, or other paranormal phenomena', using 'only recognized scientific knowledge, without speculation or unverified hypotheses'. Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/histoire-du-geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEIPAN's classification was rebuilt in 2008 on two measured parameters, consistency (C) and residual strangeness (E), and class D is 'généralement attribuée suite à une enquête terrain avec rencontre du ou des témoins'. Source: GEIPAN, 'Classification', geipan.fr, 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** GEPAN's investigation of the Trans-en-Provence physical-trace case of 8 January 1981 was published as Note Technique n° 16, 'Analyse d'une trace', CNES Toulouse, and remains hosted on the GEIPAN site. Source: https://www.cnes-geipan.fr/sites/default/files/note_tech_16.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Key milestones per CNES: '2008: GEIPAN website created and UAP archives transferred to it; 2005: GEIPAN replaces SEPRA; 1988: GEPAN replaced by SEPRA, the permanent unit at CNES responsible for routine monitoring of orbital debris and risk objects; 1977: Inception of GEPAN'. Source: https://cnes.fr/en/projects/geipan
- **Tier 1 documented.** Published statistics dated 11/08/2026 cover 3,381 classified cases: 950 class A (28.1%), 1,310 class B (38.7%), 1,015 class C (30.0%), 106 class D (3.1%). Source: https://cnes-geipan.fr/en/stats
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same report records that GEIPAN has 'a team of three full-time employees', that reports are investigated by 'over 20 trained volunteers throughout France', and that its guiding principles include 'low operational costs, public service, respect for witnesses and a commitment to transparency, neutrality and objective investigation'. Source: https://ised-isde.canada.ca/site/science/en/office-chief-science-advisor/sky-canada-project/management-public-reporting-unidentified-aerial-phenomena-canada
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Director succession: Jacques Patenet 2005-2009, Yves Blanc 2009-2011, Xavier Passot 2011-2015, Jean-Paul Aguttes 2016-2019, Roger Baldacchino 2019-2020, Vincent Costes 2021-2023, Frédéric Courtade 2024-present. Source: Wikipedia 'GEIPAN', citing CNES-GEIPAN appointment notices and The Debrief (24 April 2024)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** GEPAN's June 1978 five-volume report to its scientific council studied eleven high-credibility, high-strangeness cases; 'only one proved to have a conventional explanation'. 'In 10 of the 11 cases, the conclusion was that the witnesses had witnessed a material phenomenon that could not be explained as a natural phenomenon or a human device', and the report concluded that behind the phenomenon there is a 'flying machine. . . whose modes of sustenance and propulsion are beyond our knowledge'. Source: 'French Government UFO Study', contemporaneous English-language summary submitted by Ron Westrum, held in the NSA public document collection - https://www.nsa.gov/portals/75/documents/news-features/declassified-documents/ufo/french_gov_ufo_study.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The same summary states that in GEPAN's investigations 'the care with which distances, angles, and psychological factors were evaluated makes the bulk of the Condon Report seem very poor by comparison', and that each case was worked by a four-person team including a psychologist. Source: https://www.nsa.gov/portals/75/documents/news-features/declassified-documents/ufo/french_gov_ufo_study.pdf

### Its strongest connections

- GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés runs out to **COMETA - Comité d'Études Approfondies** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: shared IHEDN lineage; SEPRA head contributed. COMETA's introduction records the shared parentage: 'In 1976, a committee of the Institut des Hautes Etudes de Defense Nationale (IHEDN) chaired by General Blanchard of the Gendarmerie Nationale opened the unidentified flying objects file. The objective: to make proposals for organizing research and the collection of data on these phenomena. The goal was achieved, because the recommendations of this committee were followed in the creation of the Groupe d'Etude des Phenomenes Aerospatiaux Non Identifies (GEPAN).' COMETA then rests its own report on GEPAN/SEPRA's casework by name. Sources: COMETA, 'UFOs and Defense: What Should We Prepare For?', 1999, Introduction, https://ia800403.us.archive.org/17/items/pdfy-NRIQie2ooVehep7K/The%20Cometa%20Report%20%5BUFO%27s%20And%20Defense%20-%20What%20Should%20We%20Prepare%20For%5D.pdf, dated 1999-07-01.

The link reads: identifies GEIPAN as a model and recommends partnership. Canada's Office of the Chief Science Advisor examined GEIPAN in detail, told Canada that 'the example of GEIPAN in France is worth considering' when establishing a dedicated service, and recommended in recommendation 13 that Canada's lead organisation partner with 'AARO and NASA (U.S.A.), GEIPAN (France), and SEFAA (Chile)'. - GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés runs out to **France** (location, board tier C), a geographic link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: French national UAP office, CNES Toulouse. - GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés comes in from **Centre National d'Études Spatiales (CNES)** (institution, board tier C), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: GEIPAN is a unit of CNES at Toulouse. The French office has been hosted inside the national space agency continuously since 1977, an arrangement Norlain called unique in the world. - GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés runs out to **CEFAA / SEFAA - Chilean anomalous aerial phenomena office, Dirección General de Aeronáutica Civil** (institution, board tier A), a investigative link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: French analysts worked the Chilean Navy FLIR case. Photo and video analysts in France, including François Louange, were brought into CEFAA's investigation of the 11 November 2014 Chilean Navy infrared footage through GEIPAN. The two offices reached different conclusions - GEIPAN's analysts proposed a medium-haul aircraft venting, CEFAA rejected it - and both published. It is the clearest documented case of state UAP offices on two continents working the same tape. Sources: https://www.huffpost.com/entry/groundbreaking-ufo-video-just-released-from-chilean_b_586d37bce4b014e7c72ee56b, https://www.huffpost.com/entry/groundbreaking-ufo-video-just-released-from-chilean_b_586d37bce4b014e7c72ee56b; https://www.newsweek.com/chilean-navy-id-mysterious-aircraft-caught-video-539786, https://www.newsweek.com/chilean-navy-id-mysterious-aircraft-caught-video-539786
- GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés comes in from **Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: COMPARISON: publish the raw record and disclaim competence, versus publish the adjudicated case file. COMPARISON EDGE, no contact between the Arquivo Nacional and GEIPAN is claimed. Two different theories of state openness. GEIPAN is the investigating office itself publishing a searchable, four-tier-classified case database out of CNES; the Brazilian arrangement is the opposite shape, the military transfers scanned raw records into a civil archive and then says it has no specialists qualified to investigate or to offer an opinion. Brazil gives the citizen the primary document and no adjudication; France gives the citizen the adjudication. The board should not treat these as the same category of openness. Tier 2 because the comparison is analytic; the underlying facts on each side are separately sourced on the two nodes. Sources: Arquivo Nacional (Brasil), 'Conheça o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional; Office of the Chief Science Advisor of Canada, 'Management of Public Reporting of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in Canada' (Sky Canada Project, 2025), https://science.gc.ca/site/science/en/office-chief-science-advisor/sky-canada-project/management-public-reporting-unidentified-aerial-phenomena-canada; CNES - Cadastro Nacional de Estabelecimentos de Saúde, establishment record 2761041 (Ministério da Saúde open data API), https://apidadosabertos.saude.gov.br/cnes/estabelecimentos/2761041, dated 2026-08-12; Arquivo Nacional - portal, https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br, dated 2026-08-12.
- GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés comes in from **Peruvian Air Force anomalous aerial phenomena office (OIFAA / DIFAA)** (institution, board tier C), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: COMPARISON: the military-host model against the space-agency-host model. COMPARISON EDGE, no contact is claimed and none was found. DIFAA is hosted by an air force; GEIPAN is hosted by a civil space agency and publishes its case database. The pairing exists on this board to keep the national-office row honest: 'a country has an office' is not one fact but at least three different institutional arrangements, and Peru is the least documented of them. This pass did not locate DIFAA's founding legal instrument, its current reporting line, its case output, or any evidence that it publishes findings. Sources: Agencia Peruana de Noticias Andina (Editora Perú), 'Fenómenos aéreos anómalos en Perú podrán reportarse a la FAP', 18 October 2013, https://andina.pe/agencia/noticia-fenomenos-aereos-anomalos-peru-podran-reportarse-a-fap-478982.aspx; Office of the Chief Science Advisor of Canada, 'Management of Public Reporting of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in Canada' (Sky Canada Project, 2025), https://science.gc.ca/site/science/en/office-chief-science-advisor/sky-canada-project/management-public-reporting-unidentified-aerial-phenomena-canada; Andina, 'Fenomenos aereos anomalos en Peru podran reportarse a la FAP', 18 October 2013, https://andina.pe/agencia/noticia-fenomenos-aereos-anomalos-peru-podran-reportarse-a-fap-478982.aspx, dated 2013-10-18; CNES, GEIPAN project page, https://www.cnes.fr/en/projects/geipan, dated 2026-08-25.

## Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region (DIS Scientific & Technical Memorandum 55/2/00)

Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region (DIS Scientific & Technical Memorandum 55/2/00) is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2000 to 2006-05-15; axes C1 A2 D3; 9 connections; in the research band.

In one line: THREE-volume UK Defence Intelligence Staff report (not four); the separately-released standalone Executive Summary is what has been miscounted as a fourth part. Volume 3 preface is dated February 2000. Released 15 May 2006 under FOI to David Clarke and Gary Anthony.

### What it is

Project Condign was a classified UK Ministry of Defence study of unidentified aerial phenomena carried out for the Defence Intelligence Staff between 1996 and 2000, resulting in a three-volume, roughly 460-page report titled 'Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region.' It drew on approximately 10,000 UK sighting reports held by DI55, the Defence Intelligence branch responsible for UFO investigations inside the Directorate of Scientific and Technical Intelligence. Classified 'Secret UK Eyes Only,' it was released in May 2006 after a Freedom of Information Act request by researchers David Clarke and Gary Anthony.

### Why it is implicated

Condign is the clearest documented case of a NATO partner's intelligence service running a multi-year, compartmented UAP program while telling the public and Parliament almost nothing. Its debunking conclusion sits alongside an explicit recommendation that the plasma phenomenon it invoked be examined for 'novel military applications,' with the implications already briefed to MoD technology managers, a technology-exploitation tasking hiding inside a dismissal document. Most importantly, the sections the MoD fought hardest to keep censored for two decades turned out not to concern aliens at all but rumoured US black programs (Aurora/Astra and the TR-3A 'Black Manta'), and the MoD's stated justification in February 2026 was that release 'could still provide adversaries with an insight into the developments of US/UK aircraft technology.' That is a government stating on the record that UAP files are classified in order to protect black aerospace programs, and doing so on behalf of a foreign partner. Condign also contains a precise, dated, attributable negative that any retrieval hypothesis has to answer: that no artefacts of unknown origin had ever been handed to UK authorities.

### The longer account

Project Condign is the UK's answer to the question of what an intelligence service actually does with a national UFO file when it thinks nobody is watching. The MoD had, since the 1950s, maintained a nominal UFO desk that handled public correspondence. Behind it sat DI55, a section of the Directorate of Scientific and Technical Intelligence within the Defence Intelligence Staff, which quietly collected sighting reports for defence-intelligence purposes. In 1996 DI55 commissioned defence scientist Ron Haddow, whom, according to David Clarke, DI55 regarded as their subject expert, to write a comprehensive assessment. Haddow was given access to the retained MoD holdings, worked for four years, and delivered in 2000 a three-volume study running to roughly 460 pages. It was classified Secret UK Eyes Only and, by Clarke's account, shown to only a handful of people.

The report's public reputation is as a debunking. That is a fair reading of its headline conclusions: 'There is no evidence that any UAP, seen in the UKADR, are incursions by air-objects of any intelligent (extra-terrestrial or foreign) origin, or that they represent any hostile intent,' and 'No artefacts of unknown or unexplained origin have been reported or handed to the UK authorities, despite thousands of UAP reports. There are no SIGINT, ELINT or radiation measurements and little useful video or still IMINT.' Those are unusually specific negatives, and they are the strongest single piece of counter-evidence a British-side retrieval narrative has to overcome. An honest board records them as such.

But the report is not only a debunking, and this is where it becomes load-bearing for the program argument. Its residual-explanation hypothesis is 'Buoyant Plasma Formation', an atmospheric plasma phenomenon it proposes as the cause of black-triangle reports and of close-encounter perceptual effects, via electromagnetic fields that could 'adversely affect a vehicle or person.' Having proposed it, the report does not stop at explanation. It recommends further research into novel military applications of the phenomenon and records that 'the implications have already been briefed to the relevant MoD technology managers.' It also notes that scientists in the former Soviet Union had 'identified the close connection between the UFO Phenomena and Plasma technologies' and were 'pursuing related techniques for potential military purposes.' In other words: a classified UAP study concluding there is nothing to see also concluded that there was something worth weaponising, and routed it to acquisition people. That is the structural signature of an exploitation channel, and it is on the record in an executive summary the MoD itself released.

The second thread is the redactions. When Clarke received the report in 2006, handed to him in MoD Main Building by Linda Unwin, then the penultimate UFO desk officer, a post abolished in 2009, sections of volume 2 stamped NATO Secret were withheld. The MoD held that line for two decades through repeated FOI requests, invoking FOIA exemptions 24 (national security) and 27 (international relations), and as recently as February 2026 refused release on the grounds that disclosure 'could still provide adversaries with an insight into the developments of US/UK aircraft technology, compromising the military capabilities of US partners and our own Air Force assets.' Four months later the identical material was released in full to an anonymous requester. What it contained were two speculative paragraphs on the rumoured Aurora/Astra programme and on the TR-3A 'Black Manta,' with a figure annotating the latter as having a 60-foot wingspan and 42-foot length, described as 'covert survivable in weather strike/recce,' in two variants, one 'seen with sausage-shaped contrail (or smoke?)' and one 'probably/possibly with pulsating motor.' Also withheld had been head-on silhouettes of the F-117 and B-2 with the observation that their fuselage shapes 'can be reported respectively as saucer or arc/boomerang shaped under certain silhouette and lighting conditions,' and the note that 'these aircraft types are unfamiliar as they are rarely spotted in UK airspace.'

The analytic yield is twofold. First, the UK's defence intelligence establishment in the 1990s privately believed the Americans were flying triangular and diamond-shaped stealth platforms through UK airspace, with or without permission, and used that belief as an explanatory bin for domestic UAP reports, the same move AARO makes today. Second, the twenty-year classification of that material demonstrates the mechanism the board is trying to characterise: UAP secrecy sustained not because of what UAP are, but because black-program equities, including a partner nation's, attach to the same file. Clarke's own judgment on the 2006 release was that it shed no new light on the phenomenon but did show DIS had been running a far larger investigation than it had ever admitted.

What the record does not show is any Condign statement about materials, retrievals, or reverse-engineering, it explicitly denies artefacts. It says nothing about US programs beyond the Aurora and TR-3 speculation. And because the study was never peer reviewed, the plasma hypothesis has been challenged as one that would not have survived independent scrutiny.

### What it has denied

The report itself is the denial artifact: a dated, signed, government-authored statement that no UAP in UK airspace are of intelligent extra-terrestrial or foreign origin and that no unknown artefacts were ever handed to UK authorities. The MoD separately maintained for two decades, most recently in February 2026, that specific redacted sections could not be released without compromising US/UK aircraft technology, a national-security justification it abandoned four months later by releasing the same material in full.

Roles the board assigns it: research, concealment, disclosure.

### Open questions on this node

- Who inside DI55 tasked Haddow, and under what standing requirement was DI55 collecting UAP reports in the first place?
- Which 'relevant MoD technology managers' received the plasma briefing, and did any research programme follow?
- Did any US agency review, coordinate on, or object to portions of the report before or after the 2006 release, and is there a US-side copy?
- What is in volume 3, and does the surviving original three-volume copy the MoD rediscovered (per Clarke's 2023 'Lost and Found' post) differ from the 2006 release?
- Does the Condign statement that no artefacts were ever handed to UK authorities extend to materials held by UK contractors or transferred to US custody, or only to direct handovers to the MoD?

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 95 of 1057, score 64). 9 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The report notes that scientists in the former Soviet Union 'have identified the close connection between the UFO Phenomena and Plasma technologies' and are 'pursuing related techniques for potential military purposes' (Executive Summary, pp. 9-10). Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Condign
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The report states: 'No artefacts of unknown or unexplained origin have been reported or handed to the UK authorities, despite thousands of UAP reports. There are no SIGINT, ELINT or radiation measurements and little useful video or still IMINT.' Source: http://www.ianridpath.com/ufo/condign.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The report was released into the public domain on 15 May 2006 following a September 2005 Freedom of Information Act request by David Clarke of Sheffield Hallam University and Gary Anthony, a former BUFORA astronomical consultant. Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Condign

The link reads: UK-commissioned study of its own air defence region. The report is DI55's, and its title fixes its scope: 'Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region', completed in 2000. From that point onwards DI55 was removed from the distribution list of UFO reports received by the MoD. It is a state-commissioned assessment of the phenomenon over British territory with no American equivalent released to the public. The link reads: Condign report held at MoD Main Building. DI55, which produced the report, is a Defence Intelligence branch of the Ministry of Defence; it inherited responsibility for inquiries into UFO incidents of possible defence significance in 1967. The National Archives guide places the MoD's UFO secretariat work at Whitehall but does not state where the Condign volumes were physically held, so the Whitehall tie here is institutional rather than a documented location of the report.
